\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)=\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}+\int_{t}^{s}\mathcal{U}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau,\\
&l_{vx}(X_{Xt}(s),u_{Xt}(s))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)+l_{vv}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{\mathcal{U}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)+\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)=0,\\
&-d\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)=\Big[l_{xx}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)+l_{xv}(X_{Xt}(s),u_{Xt}(s))\mathcal{\mathcal{U}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\\
&\qquad\qquad+D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})\right)(\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s))\Big]ds-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathcal{R}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)dw_{j}(s)-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Theta_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)db_{j}(s),\\
&\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T)=h_{xx}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T)+D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})\right)(\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T)).\end{aligned}\right.

The random variables
\mathcal{\mathcal{U}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
,
\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
,
\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
,
\mathcal{R}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)
,
\Theta_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)
are
\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{s}=\sigma(X_{\cdot},\mathcal{X}_{\cdot})\cup\mathcal{F}_{t}^{s}
measurable.
They belong to
L_{\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}}^{2}(t,T;\mathcal{H}_{m}),

where
\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}
is the filtration generated
by the
\sigma
-algebras
\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{s}
.
Then

(7.11)

D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(\mathcal{X}_{\cdot})=\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(t).

The proof can be found in Appendix C.

7.3.
CASE WHEN
\mathcal{X}
IS INDEPENDENT OF
X

In this section, we consider the characterization of
D_{X}^{2}V(X\otimes m,t)(\mathcal{X})

when
\mathcal{X}
is independent of
X.
It is assumed that
\mathcal{X}

is independent of the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{t}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{X}

is also independent of the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{Xt}.
We then have the following proposition:

Proposition 7.4
.

We make the assumptions of Proposition
7.3

and
\mathcal{X}
independent of the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{Xt}
,
with
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X})=0
. Then the system (
7.10
) becomes

(7.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)=\mathcal{X}+\int_{t}^{s}\mathcal{U}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau,

(7.13)

\displaystyle l_{vx}(X_{Xt}(s),u_{Xt}(s))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)+l_{vv}(X_{Xt}(s),u_{Xt}(s))\mathcal{\mathcal{U}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)+\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)=0,

\displaystyle-d\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)=\Big[\Big(l_{xx}(X_{Xt}(s),u_{Xt}(s))+D^{2}\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{Xt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{Xt}(s))\Big)\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)

(7.14)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+l_{xv}(X_{Xt}(s),u_{Xt}(s))\mathcal{\mathcal{U}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\Big]ds-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathcal{R}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)dw_{j}(s)-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Theta_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)db_{j}(s),

(7.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T)=\left(h_{xx}(X_{Xt}(T))+D^{2}\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}((X_{Xt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X_{Xt}(T))\right)\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T).

Proof.

It is sufficient to show that

(7.16)

D^{2}\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{Xt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})\mathcal{X}_{X\mathcal{X}t}(s)=D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X_{Xt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})\right)(\mathcal{X}_{X\mathcal{X}t}(s)).

From formula (
2.39
), we have

(7.17)

\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{Xt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{Xt}(s),X_{X_{\cdot}^{1}t}^{1}(s))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}^{1}\mathcal{\chi}^{1}t}^{1}(s)dm(x^{1})\right)=0

since
\mathcal{\chi}^{1}
is independent of the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{X^{1}t}

and
\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}^{1}\mathcal{\chi}^{1}t}^{1}(s)
can be written
as
\mathcal{X}_{X^{1}t}^{1}(s)(\mathcal{\chi}^{1})
, where
\mathcal{X}_{X^{1}t}^{1}(s)(\cdot)\in L_{\mathcal{F}_{X^{1}t}}^{2}(t,T;\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{m};\mathcal{H}_{m})).

So
\mathcal{\chi}^{1}
is independent of
X_{X_{\cdot}^{1}t}^{1}(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{X}_{X^{1}t}^{1}(\cdot)(\cdot).
Therefore, the left hand side
of (
7.17
) is

\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{Xt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{Xt}(s),X_{X_{\cdot}^{1}t}^{1}(s))\mathcal{X}_{X^{1}t}^{1}(s)(\mathbb{E}^{1}(\mathcal{\chi}^{1}))dm(x^{1})\right)=0

since
\mathbb{E}^{1}(\mathcal{\chi}^{1})=0.

∎

7.4.
SECOND-ORDER FUNCTIONAL DERIVATIVE OF THE VALUE FUNCTION

We want to compute the second-order functional derivative of the value
function
\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1}).
We know that it
is sufficient to obtain the second gradient
D_{1}D_{2}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1}).

We can use the formula (
2.23
). If
x\mapsto y_{x}
is
in
L_{m}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{n})
then we can write

(7.18)

D_{X}^{2}V(m,t)(y_{\cdot})=D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)y_{x}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})y_{x^{1}}dm(x^{1}).

Consider
\mathcal{\mathcal{U}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
,
\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
,
\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
,
\mathcal{R}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)
,
\Theta_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{j}(s)

with
X_{\cdot}=J_{\cdot},\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}=y_{\cdot}.
Since they represent the
value of random linear operators from
L_{m}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
into itself, we shall represent them with kernels denoted
u_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)
,
X_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)
,
Z_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)
,
r_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)
,
\rho_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s).

In particular,

(7.19)

(D_{X}^{2}V(m,t)(y_{\cdot}))_{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}Z_{mt}(x,x^{1},t)y_{x^{1}}dm(x^{1}).

We need to introduce the identity operator on
L_{m}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
represented formally by the kernel
I_{m}(x,x^{1}),
so that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}I_{m}(x,x^{1})y_{x^{1}}dm(x^{1})=y_{x}.

Formally
I_{m}(x,x^{1})=\delta(x-x^{1}).

The system (
7.10
)
yields

(7.20)

\displaystyle X_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)=I_{m}(x,x^{1})+\int_{t}^{s}u_{mt}(x,x^{1},\tau)d\tau,

(7.21)

\displaystyle l_{vx}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))X_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)+l_{vv}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))u_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)+Z_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)=0,

(7.22)

\displaystyle-dZ_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)=\Bigg[l_{xx}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))X_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)+l_{xv}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))u_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+D^{2}\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{xmt}(s))X_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{xmt}(s),X_{\eta mt}^{1}(s))X_{mt}^{1}(\eta,x^{1},s)dm(\eta)\right)\Bigg]ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{j=1}^{n}r_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)dw_{j}(s)-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\rho_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)db_{j}(s),

\displaystyle Z_{mt}(x,x^{1},T)=h_{xx}(X_{xmt}(T),u_{xmt}(T))X_{mt}(x,x^{1},T)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+D^{2}\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X_{xmt}(T))X_{mt}(x,x^{1},T)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F_{T}}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X_{xmt}(T),X_{\eta mt}^{1}(T))X_{mt}^{1}(\eta,x^{1},T)dm(\eta)\right).

From (
7.11
), (
7.18
), and (
7.19
), we can write

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}Z_{mt}(x,x^{1},t)y_{x^{1}}dm(x^{1})

\displaystyle=D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)y_{x}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})y_{x^{1}}dm(x^{1})

(7.23)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{xmt}(t)y_{x}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})y_{x^{1}}dm(x^{1}).

So we obtain the formula:

(7.24)

Z_{mt}(x,x^{1},t)=\mathcal{Z}_{xmt}(t)I_{m}(x,x^{1})+DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1}).

We introduce, more generally,

(7.25)

\displaystyle\overline{X}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle:=X_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)-\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(s)I_{m}(x,x^{1}),

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle:=u_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)-\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)I_{m}(x,x^{1}),

\displaystyle\overline{Z}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle:=Z_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)-\mathcal{Z}_{xmt}(s)I_{m}(x,x^{1}),

\displaystyle\overline{r}_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle:=r_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)-\mathcal{R}_{xmt}^{j}(s)I_{m}(x,x^{1}),

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle:=\rho_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)-\Theta_{xmt}^{j}(s)I_{m}(x,x^{1}).

Combining (
7.20
), (
7.21
), (
7.22
)
with (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
) we obtain the system:

(7.26)

\displaystyle\overline{X}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)=\int_{t}^{s}\overline{u}_{mt}(x,x^{1},\tau)d\tau,

(7.27)

\displaystyle l_{vx}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))\overline{X}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)+l_{vv}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))\overline{u}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)+\overline{Z}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)=0,

(7.28)

\displaystyle-d\overline{Z}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)=\Bigg[l_{xx}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))\overline{X}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)+l_{xv}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))\overline{u}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+D^{2}\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{xmt}(s))\overline{X}_{mt}(x,x^{1},s)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{xmt}(s),X_{\eta mt}^{1}(s))\overline{X}_{mt}^{1}(\eta,x^{1},s)dm(\eta)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\left(DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{xmt}(s),X_{x^{1}mt}^{1}(s))\right)\Bigg]ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\overline{r}_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)dw_{j}(s)-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\overline{\rho}_{mt}^{j}(x,x^{1},s)db_{j}(s),

\displaystyle\overline{Z}_{mt}(x,x^{1},T)=h_{xx}(X_{xmt}(T),u_{xmt}(T))\overline{X}_{mt}(x,x^{1},T)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+D^{2}\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X_{xmt}(T))\overline{X}_{mt}(x,x^{1},T)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F_{T}}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X_{xmt}(T),X_{\eta mt}^{1}(T))\overline{X}_{mt}^{1}(\eta,x^{1},T)dm(\eta)\right)

(7.29)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\left(DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F_{T}}{d\nu^{2}}((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X_{xmt}(T),X_{x^{1}mt}^{1}(T))\right),

and we have

(7.30)

\overline{Z}_{mt}(x,x^{1},t)=D_{1}D_{2}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1}).

Using the estimate (
7.8
) and the relation (
7.18
)
we can state

(7.31)

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})y_{x}\cdot y_{x^{1}}dm(x)dm(x^{1})\right|\leq C_{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|y_{x}|^{2}dm(x).

Since
y_{x}
is arbitrary, we can state

(7.32)

\left|DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})\right|\leq C_{T}.

A rigorous proof can be obtained by using the system (
7.26
),
(
7.27
), (
7.28
), (
7.29
), and proceeding
as in the proof of Proposition
7.2
. As a consequence
the function
(x,m)\mapsto D\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)
is continuous.
From (
5.7
) we also know that the function
t\mapsto D\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)

is continuous.

7.5.
BELLMAN EQUATION

We can now state the following:

Theorem 7.5
.

We make the assumptions of Proposition
6.1
, the value function
V(X\otimes m,t)
is solution of the Bellman equation:

(7.33)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\partial V}{\partial t}(X\otimes m,t)+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}H(X_{\cdot},D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))\,dm(x)\right)+F(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)\\
&\qquad+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\right\rangle+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e^{j}),e^{j}\right\rangle=0,\ \text{a.e. }\ t;\\
&V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,T)=\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}h(X_{x})dm(x)\right)+F_{T}(X_{\cdot}\otimes m),\end{aligned}\right.

where
N_{t}
is a standard Gaussian variable in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
independent
of
X_{\cdot}
. Among functions which satisfy the regularity properties
of the value function, it is the only one solution.

The proof can be found in Appendix C.

7.6.
BELLMAN EQUATION AT
X=J

When
X_{\cdot}=J_{\cdot}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{X}V(m,t)

\displaystyle=D\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x),

\displaystyle D_{X}^{2}V(m,t)(Y_{\cdot})

\displaystyle=D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)Y_{x}+\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})Y_{x^{1}}^{1}dm(x^{1})\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle D_{X}^{2}V(m,t)(\sigma N_{t})

\displaystyle=D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)\sigma N_{t},

\displaystyle D_{X}^{2}V(m,t)(e^{j})

\displaystyle=D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)e^{j}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})e^{j}dm(x^{1}),

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\text{tr}\left(D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)dm(x),

\displaystyle\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e^{j}),e^{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Delta\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)dm(x)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})dm(x)dm(x^{1})\right).

The Bellman equation reads:

(7.34)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\partial V}{\partial t}(m,t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}H\left(x,D\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)\right)dm(x)+F(m)+\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\text{tr}\left(D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)dm(x)\\
&\qquad+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Delta\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x)dm(x)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1})dm(x)dm(x^{1})\right)=0,\\
&V(m,T)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}h(x)dm(x)+F_{T}(m).\end{aligned}\right.

8.
THE MASTER EQUATION

8.1.
OBTAINING THE MASTER EQUATION

The Bellman equation (
7.33
) links the value function
V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)

and its first- and second-order gradients
D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)
,
D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)
, as well as the time derivative
\dfrac{\partial}{\partial t}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t).

It is a partial differential equation defined on
\mathcal{H}_{m}\times(0,T).
Recall that
(X_{\cdot},t)\mapsto D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)
takes
\mathcal{H}_{m}\times(0,T)

into itself, with
D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)
being
\sigma(X)
-measurable.
Similarly, the mapping
(X_{\cdot},t)\mapsto D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)
takes

\mathcal{H}_{m}\times(0,T)
into
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{m})
,
where
D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(Y_{\cdot})\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
is
\sigma(X_{\cdot},Y_{\cdot})
-measurable.

It is well-known that with the Bellman equation, there exists an equation
for the gradient of the value function that does not include
the value function itself. This equation is derived by taking the gradient
of the Bellman equation. Rather than being a scalar equation, it forms a system
of equations. This feature also applies to the Bellman equation (
7.33
).
By taking the gradient in
X_{\cdot}
formally we obtain:

(8.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\partial}{\partial t}D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)+H_{x}(X_{\cdot},D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))+D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(H_{p}(X_{\cdot},D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)))+D_{X}F(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\dfrac{1}{2}D_{X}\,\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\right\rangle+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{X}\ \left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e^{j}),e^{j}\right\rangle=0,\ \text{a.e. }\ t,\\
&D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,T)=h_{x}(X_{x})+D_{X}F_{T}(X_{\cdot}\otimes m),\end{aligned}\right.

where
N_{t}
is a standard Gaussian random variable independent of
X_{\cdot}

8.2.
DEVELOPED FORMULAS

The key function in the following formulas is

(8.2)

U(x,m,t)=\dfrac{d}{d\nu}V(m,t)(x).

We then consider the functional derivative of
U(x,m,t),
namely

(8.3)

\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})=\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1}),

and also the second-order functional derivative:

(8.4)

\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}U(x,m,t)(x^{1},x^{2})=\dfrac{d^{3}}{d\nu^{3}}V(m,t)(x,x^{1},x^{2}).

We shall use also the notation
D
for the gradient in
x,

D_{1}

for the gradient with respect to
x^{1}
and
D_{2}
for the gradient
with respect to
x^{2}.
The variables
x,x^{1},x^{2}
may not be
listed in that order. Let the reader not confuse
D_{2}
with
D^{2}.
According
to formula (
2.22
) we thus have:

(8.5)

\displaystyle\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(Y_{\cdot}),Y_{\cdot}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D^{2}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)Y_{x}\cdot Y_{x}dm(x)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,D_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})Y_{x^{1}}^{1}\cdot Y_{x}dm(x^{1})\right)dm(x)\right),

where
X_{x^{1}}^{1},
Y_{x^{1}}^{1}
is an independent copy of

X_{x},Y_{x}
.

We first take
Y_{\cdot}=\sigma N_{t}
, to obtain, since
N_{t}
is independent
of
X_{\cdot}
,

(8.6)

\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\right\rangle=\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\text{tr}\left(D^{2}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)dm(x)\right)

and also

(8.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e_{j}),e_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Delta U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)dm(x)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})dm(x^{1})\right)dm(x)\right).

8.3.
FURTHER COMPUTATIONS

We can then compute

\displaystyle\bigg\langle D_{X}\Big(\Big\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}

\displaystyle\otimes m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\Big\rangle\Big),Z_{\cdot}\bigg\rangle=\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,(\text{tr}(D^{2}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)\sigma\sigma^{*}))\cdot Z_{x}dm(x)\right)

(8.8)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\,\text{tr}\left(D_{1}^{2}\,\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x^{1}}^{1},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x})\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)\right)\cdot Z_{x}dm_{1}(x)\right)dm(x)\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle D_{X}\Big(\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\right\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle=D\,(\text{tr}(D^{2}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)\sigma\sigma^{*}))

(8.9)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\,\text{tr}\left(D_{1}^{2}\,\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)\right)dm(x^{1})\right).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle D_{X}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Big\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e_{j}),e_{j}\Big\rangle\Bigg),Z_{\cdot}\Bigg\rangle=\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D(\Delta U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))\cdot Z_{x}dm(x)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\Delta_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)\cdot Z_{x}dm(x^{1})\right)dm(x)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)\cdot Z_{x}dm(x^{1})\right)dm(x)\right)

(8.10)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{1j}D_{2j}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}U(X_{x^{1}}^{1},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{2}}^{2},X_{x})\right)\cdot Z_{x}dm(x^{2})\right)dm(x^{1})\right)dm(x)\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle D_{X}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e_{j}),e_{j}\right\rangle\Bigg)=D(\Delta U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\Delta_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)dm(x^{1})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)dm(x^{1})\right)

(8.11)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{1j}D_{2j}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}U(X_{x^{1}}^{1},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{2}}^{2},X_{x})\right)dm(x^{2})\right)dm(x^{1})\right).

8.4.
MASTER EQUATION

Going back to (
8.1
) we first note, with formula (
8.5
),

\displaystyle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(H_{p}(X_{\cdot},D_{X}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)))=D^{2}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)H_{p}(X_{x},DU(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))

(8.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(D_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)H_{p}(X_{x^{1}}^{1},DU(X_{x^{1}}^{1},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))dm(x^{1})\right).

Collecting results, the master equation (
8.1
) yields:

(8.13)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\partial}{\partial t}DU(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)+DH(X_{x},DU(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))\\
&+D^{2}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)H_{p}(X_{x},DU(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(X_{x})\\
&+\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(D_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)H_{p}(X_{x^{1}}^{1},DU(X_{x^{1}}^{1},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))dm(x^{1})\right)\\
&+\dfrac{1}{2}D\,(\text{tr}(D^{2}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)\sigma\sigma^{*}))+\dfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\,\text{tr}\left(D_{1}^{2}\,\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)\right)dm(x^{1})\right)\\
&+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}D(\Delta U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t))+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\Delta_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)dm(x^{1})\right)\\
&+\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{1}}^{1})\right)dm(x^{1})\right)\\
&+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{1j}D_{2j}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}U(X_{x^{1}}^{1},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(X_{x^{2}}^{2},X_{x})\right)dm(x^{2})\right)dm(x^{1})\right)=0,\\
&DU(X_{x},X_{\cdot}\otimes m,T)=Dh(X_{x})+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(X_{x}).\end{aligned}\right.

8.5.
THE CASE
X_{\cdot}=J_{\cdot}

In the case
X_{\cdot}=J_{\cdot}
, we obtain:

(8.14)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\partial}{\partial t}DU(x,m,t)+DH(x,DU(x,m,t))+D^{2}U(x,m,t)H_{p}(x,DU(x,m,t))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}(m)(x)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(D_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})\right)H_{p}(x^{1},DU(x^{1},m,t))dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\dfrac{1}{2}D\,(\text{tr}(D^{2}U(x,m,t)\sigma\sigma^{*}))+\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\,\text{tr}\left(D_{1}^{2}\,\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)\right)dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}D(\Delta U(x,m,t))+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\left(\Delta_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})\right)dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\beta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})\right)dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D\,\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{1j}D_{2j}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}U(x^{1},m,t)(x^{2},x)\right)dm(x^{2})dm(x^{1})=0,\\
&DU(x,m,T)=Dh(x)+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}(m)(x).\end{aligned}\right.

We recognize that (
8.14
) is the gradient of a scalar equation, which is the equation for
U(x,m,t)
, i.e.

(8.15)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\partial}{\partial t}U(x,m,t)+H(x,DU(x,m,t))+\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}(m)(x)\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}D_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})H_{p}(x^{1},DU(x^{1},m,t))dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\dfrac{1}{2}\text{tr}(D^{2}U(x,m,t)\sigma\sigma^{*})+\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\,\text{tr}\left(D_{1}^{2}\,\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})\sigma\sigma^{*}\right)\right)dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\Delta U(x,m,t)+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Delta_{1}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\beta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{j}D_{1j}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}U(x,m,t)(x^{1})\right)dm(x^{1})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\dfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}D_{1j}D_{2j}\dfrac{d^{2}}{d\nu^{2}}U(x,m,t)(x^{1},x^{2})\right)dm(x^{2})dm(x^{1})=0,\\
&U(x,m,T)=h(x)+\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}(m)(x).\end{aligned}\right.

Equation (
8.15
) is commonly called the Master equation.

8.6.
JUSTIFICATION

The above calculations are justified if the functions
X_{\cdot}\mapsto\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\rangle

and
X_{\cdot}\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e_{j}),e_{j}\rangle

have a G
\textroundcap
ateaux differential. We need additional regularity
assumptions. We assume:

(8.16)

l_{xxx}(x,v),l_{xxv}(x,v),l_{xvv}(x,v),l_{vvv}(x,v)\ \text{ continuous and bounded},

(8.17)

(x,m)\mapsto D^{3}\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}(m)(x),\ \text{ continuous and bounded},

(8.18)

(x,m)\mapsto D^{2}D_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}(m)(x,x^{1}),DD_{1}^{2}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}(m)(x,x^{1}),\text{ continuous and bounded},

(8.19)

(x,m)\mapsto DD_{1}D_{2}\dfrac{d^{3}F}{d\nu^{3}}(m)(x,x^{1},x^{2}),\ \text{ continuous and bounded}.

We then have:

Proposition 8.1
.

We make the assumptions
7.5
and (
8.16
), (
8.17
), (
8.18
), (
8.19
).
Then the functions
X_{\cdot}\mapsto\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(\sigma N_{t}),\sigma N_{t}\rangle

and
X_{\cdot}\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle D_{X}^{2}V(X_{\cdot}\otimes m,t)(e_{j}),e_{j}\rangle

have a G
\textroundcap
ateaux differential.

The proof can be found in Appendix D.

Appendix A
PROOFS FROM SECTION
4

A.1.
PROOF OF LEMMA
4.1

Consider a control
v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)+\theta\tilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)
,
the corresponding state is
X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)+\theta\int_{t}^{s}\tilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau.

We first check that

(A.1)

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{d\theta}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{Xt}(\cdot)+\theta\tilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))\bigg|_{\theta=0}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\langle l_{v}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)),\tilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\rangle ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{{}^{s}}})\right),\int_{t}^{s}\widetilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left\langle h_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{{}^{T}}})\right),\int_{t}^{T}\widetilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle\,.

Then

\displaystyle\quad\,\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{{}^{s}}})\right),\int_{t}^{s}\widetilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left\langle h_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{{}^{T}}})\right),\int_{t}^{s}\widetilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle l_{x}(X_{\eta t}(s),v_{\eta t}(s))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot,t}(s)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{\eta t}(s)),\int_{t}^{s}\widetilde{v}_{\eta t}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle\right]d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\eta)ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle h_{x}(X_{\eta t}(T))+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot,t}(T)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X_{\eta t}(T)),\int_{t}^{T}\widetilde{v}_{\eta t}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle\right]d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\eta)\,.

Define
\Gamma_{\eta t}
by

(A.2)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\eta t}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\left(l_{x}(X_{\eta t}(s),v_{\eta t}(s))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(s)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(s))\right)ds

\displaystyle+h_{x}(X_{\eta t}(T))+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(T)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(T)).

\Gamma_{\eta t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{T}
-measurable. By the martingale
representation theorem we can write

(A.3)

\mathbb{E}(\Gamma_{\eta t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}^{s})=\mathbb{E}(\Gamma_{\eta t})+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{t}^{s}r_{\eta t}^{j}(\tau)dw^{j}(\tau)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{t}^{s}\rho_{\eta t}^{j}(\tau)db^{j}(\tau).

Define

(A.4)

Z_{\eta t}(s):=\mathbb{E}(\Gamma_{\eta t}|\mathcal{F}_{t}^{s})-\int_{t}^{s}\left(l_{x}(X_{\eta t}(\tau),v_{\eta t}(\tau))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(\tau)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\tau}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(\tau))\right)d\tau.

Then
Z_{\eta t}(s)
is unique solution of the backward SDE:

(A.5)

\hskip-14.22636pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &-dZ_{\eta t}(s)=\left(l_{x}(X_{\eta t}(s),v_{\eta t}(s))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(s)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(s))\right)ds-\sum_{j=1}^{n}r_{\eta t}^{j}(s)dw^{j}(s)-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\rho_{\eta t}^{j}(s)db^{j}(s),\\
&Z_{\eta t}(T)=h_{x}(X_{\eta t}(T))+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(T)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(T)),\end{aligned}\right.

and

(A.6)

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(l_{x}(X_{\eta t}(s),v_{\eta t}(s))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot,t}(s)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X_{\eta t}(s))\right)\cdot\int_{t}^{s}\widetilde{v}_{\eta t}(\tau)d\tau\right]d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\eta)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(h_{x}(X_{\eta t}(T))+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(T)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(T))\right)\cdot\int_{t}^{T}\widetilde{v}_{\eta t}(\tau)d\tau\right]d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\left(Z_{\eta t}(s)\cdot\widetilde{v}_{\eta t}(s)\right)d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\eta)ds=\int_{t}^{T}\langle Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),\widetilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\rangle ds.

Therefore

\dfrac{d}{d\theta}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}(\cdot)+\theta\tilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))\bigg|_{\theta=0}=\int_{t}^{T}\langle l_{v}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))+Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),\tilde{v}_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\rangle ds,

which implies the result (
4.17
).

A.2.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
4.2

We take two controls
v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}
,
v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}
. We are
going to check that

\displaystyle\quad\,\int_{t}^{T}\bigg\langle D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}(\cdot))(s)-D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}(\cdot))(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}(s)-v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}(s)\bigg\rangle ds

(A.7)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\lambda-T(c^{\prime}_{T}+c^{\prime}_{h})-\dfrac{(c^{\prime}+c^{\prime}_{l})}{2}T^{2}\right)\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}(s)-v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds,

and from the assumption (
4.19
) the result will follow immediateley.
To simplify notation, we set
v^{1}(s)=v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}(s),\ v^{2}(s)=v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}(s)

and

X^{1}(s)=X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s;v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}(\cdot)),\ X^{2}(s)=X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s;v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}(\cdot)),

and
Z^{1}(s),Z^{2}(s)
for the corresponding solutions of (
4.18
).

From formula (
4.17
) we have

\displaystyle\quad\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}(\cdot))(s)-D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}(\cdot))(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{1}(s)-v_{X_{\cdot}t}^{2}(s)\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle l_{v}(X^{1}(s),v^{1}(s))-l_{v}(X^{2}(s),v^{2}(s)),v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)\right\rangle ds+\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle Z^{1}(s)-Z^{2}(s),v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)\right\rangle ds.

Next, since
v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)=\dfrac{d}{ds}(X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s))

and
X^{1}(t)-X^{2}(t)=0,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle Z^{1}(s)-Z^{2}(s),v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle=\left\langle Z^{1}(T)-Z^{2}(T),X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}\bigg\langle l_{x}(X^{1}(s),v^{1}(s))-l_{x}(X^{2}(s),v^{2}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X^{1}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})\right)

\displaystyle-D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X^{2}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})\right),X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\bigg\rangle ds.

Therefore, using the assumptions (
4.8
), (
4.9
), (
4.15
), (
4.16
)
we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v^{1}(\cdot))(s)-D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v^{2}(\cdot))(s),v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds-(c^{\prime}_{l}+c^{\prime})\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds-(c^{\prime}_{T}+c^{\prime}_{h})\left\lVert X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)\right\rVert^{2}.

We next use

X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)=\int_{t}^{s}(v^{1}(\tau)-v^{2}(\tau))d\tau,

hence

\displaystyle\left\lVert X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq T\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{T^{2}}{2}\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert v^{1}(s)-v^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds.

Collecting results, we obtain easily (
A.7
) and the convexity
is proven. From (
A.7
), it also follows that

(A.8)

\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))(s)-D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(0)(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle ds\geq c_{0}\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds.

But

J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))-J_{X_{\cdot}t}(0)=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(\theta v_{X_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle dsd\theta,

and combining with (
A.8
) we obtain

J_{X_{\cdot}t}(v_{X_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))-J_{X_{\cdot}t}(0)\geq\int_{t}^{T}\langle D_{v}J_{X_{\cdot}t}(0)(s),v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\rangle ds+\dfrac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{t}^{T}||v_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)||^{2}ds,

which implies the coercivity, and the existence and uniqueness of
a minimum of
J_{Xt}(v_{Xt}(\cdot)).
This completes the proof.

A.3.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
4.3

We begin by writing the equivalent of (
4.21
) for a problem
where the initial conditions
X_{x},t
are replaced with
X_{X_{x}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon,

conditioning with respect to
\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}.
The
optimal control is
u_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)
and
the optimal state is
X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)
defined
by:

(A.9)

X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)=X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)+\int_{t+\epsilon}^{s}u_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(\tau)d\tau+\sigma(w(s)-w(t+\epsilon))+\beta(b(s)-b(t+\epsilon)).

Also
X_{\cdot}\otimes m
must be replaced with
(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}.

The condition is then the following:

\displaystyle\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[l_{v}(X_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(s),u_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(s))+\int_{s}^{T}\Bigg(l_{x}(X_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(\tau),u_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(\tau))\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left(\left(X_{\cdot,t+\epsilon}(\tau)\otimes(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}\right)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{\tau}}\right)(X_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(\tau))\Bigg)d\tau+h_{x}(X_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(T))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left.D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\left(\left(X_{\cdot,t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}\right)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{T}}\right)(X_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(T))\right]

(A.10)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\widetilde{v}_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(s)d\left((X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}\right)(\eta)\Bigg)ds=0.

By the independence of
\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}
and
X_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(s),u_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(s)

this can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Bigg[l_{v}(X_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s))+\int_{s}^{T}\Bigg(l_{x}(X_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(\tau),u_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(\tau))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left(\left(X_{\cdot,t+\epsilon}(\tau)\otimes(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}\right)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{\tau}}\right)(X_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(\tau))\Bigg)d\tau+h_{x}(X_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(T))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\left(\left(X_{\cdot,t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}\right)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{T}}\right)(X_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(T))\Bigg]

(A.11)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\widetilde{v}_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\eta)\Bigg)ds=0.

If we consider the control
u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)
, for which the corresponding
state is
X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)
, we first check

(A.12)

\left(X_{\cdot,t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}\right)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}=(X_{\cdot,t}(s)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}},\quad\forall s>t+\epsilon.

Indeed for a continuous bounded test function
\varphi(\xi)
we have

\displaystyle\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(\xi)d\left(X_{\cdot,t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}\right)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(X_{\eta,t+\epsilon}(s))d(X_{\cdot,t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}}(\eta)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{{}^{t+\epsilon}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(X_{X_{\zeta t}^{1}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s))d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\zeta)\right)\right],

where, given
\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}
,
X_{\zeta t}^{1}(t+\epsilon)

is an independent copy of
X_{\zeta t}(t+\epsilon)
and the conditional
expectation
\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{{}^{t+\epsilon}}}
is taken with
respect to this copy. We can then interchange the conditional expectations to simplify it to

\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{{}^{t+\epsilon}}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(X_{X_{\zeta t}^{1}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s))d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\zeta)\right)\right],

and from the independence of
X_{\xi,t+\epsilon}(s)
and
X_{\zeta t}(t+\epsilon
)
for fixed
\xi,\zeta
, we finally can write the integral in question as

\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(X_{\zeta t}(s))d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\zeta)\right),

which proves (
A.12
). But then (
A.11
) becomes:

\displaystyle\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Bigg[l_{v}(X_{\eta t}(s),u_{\eta t}(s))+\int_{s}^{T}\Bigg(l_{x}(X_{\eta t}(\tau),u_{\eta t}(\tau))+D\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(\tau)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\tau}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(\tau))\Bigg)d\tau

(A.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+h_{x}(X_{\eta t}(T))+D\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot,t}(T)\otimes(X_{\cdot}\otimes m))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X_{\eta t}(T))\Bigg]\cdot\widetilde{v}_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)d(X_{\cdot}\otimes m)(\eta)\Bigg)ds=0,

and this is true, by taking in (
4.21
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{\eta t}(s)=

\displaystyle 0,

if
t<s<t+\epsilon
;

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{\eta t}(s)=

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{X_{\eta t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s),

if
s>t+\epsilon
.

This completes the proof.

Appendix B
PROOFS FROM SECTION
5

B.1.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
5.1

To simplify notation, we omit the indices
X_{\cdot}t
in
X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s).

We have

\displaystyle\quad\dfrac{d}{ds}\langle Z(s),X(s)-\sigma(w(s)-w(t))-\beta(b(s)-b(t))\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle Z(s),u(s)\rangle-\bigg\langle l_{x}(X(s),u(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})\right),X(s)-\sigma(w(s)-w(t))-\beta(b(s)-b(t))\bigg\rangle.

Integrating between
t
and
T,
we obtain

\displaystyle\langle X,Z(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\langle l_{v}(X(s),u(s)),u(s)\rangle ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle l_{x}(X(s),u(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right),X(s)-\sigma(w(s)-w(t))-\beta(b(s)-b(t))\right\rangle ds

(B.1)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left\langle h_{x}(X(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right),X(T)-\sigma(w(T)-w(t))-\beta(b(T)-b(t)\right\rangle.

We also have

(B.2)

\displaystyle\langle X,Z(t)\rangle=

\displaystyle\left\langle X,\int_{t}^{T}\bigg(l_{x}(X(s),u(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)\bigg)ds\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle X,h_{x}(X(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right)\right\rangle.

Therefore,

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\langle l_{v}(X(s),u(s)),u(s)\rangle ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle l_{x}(X(s),u(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right),X(s)-X-\sigma(w(s)-w(t))-\beta(b(s)-b(t))\right\rangle ds

(B.3)

\displaystyle+\left\langle h_{x}(X(T))+\ D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right),X(T)-X-\sigma(w(T)-w(t))-\beta(b(T)-b(t))\right\rangle\,.

From the assumptions (
4.5
), (
4.6
), (
4.8
), (
4.9
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
), (
4.13
), (
4.15
), (
4.16
) we obtain, after
easy majorations

(B.4)

0\geq\left(\lambda-(c^{\prime}_{l}+c^{\prime})\dfrac{T^{2}}{2}-(c^{\prime}_{h}+c^{\prime}_{T})T\right)\int_{t}^{T}||u(s)||^{2}ds-C_{T}(1+||X_{\cdot}||)\left(\int_{t}^{T}||u(s)||^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Thanks to (
4.19
), we obtain

(B.5)

\int_{t}^{T}||u(s)||^{2}ds\leq C_{T}(1+||X_{\cdot}||^{2}),

therefore

(B.6)

\sup_{s\in(t,T)}||X(s)||\leq C_{T}(1+||X||).

From (
4.18
) we can check that

\displaystyle||Z(s)||^{2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{s}^{T}\left\langle Z(\tau),l_{x}(X(\tau),u(\tau))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\tau}}\right)\right)\ \right\rangle d\tau

(B.7)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left\lVert h_{x}(X(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{\tau}}\right)\right)\right\rVert^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{s}^{T}||r^{j}(\tau)||^{2}d\tau-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{s}^{T}||\rho^{j}(\tau)||^{2}d\tau,

from which it follows immediately that

(B.8)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sup_{s\in(t,T)}||Z(s)||\leq C_{T}(1+||X||),\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{s}^{T}||r^{j}(\tau)||^{2}d\tau+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{s}^{T}||\rho^{j}(\tau)||^{2}d\tau\leq C_{T}(1+||X||^{2}).\end{aligned}\right.

From (
4.20
) and the assumptions (
4.5
), (
4.8
)
it follows also that

(B.9)

\sup_{s\in(t,T)}||u(s)||\leq C_{T}(1+||X||),

which completes the proof.

B.2.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
5.2

Let
X_{\cdot}^{1},X_{\cdot}^{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, independent of
\mathcal{W}_{t}.

We consider the systems of necessary and sufficient conditions (
4.20
) corresponding to the initial
conditions
X_{\cdot}=X_{\cdot}^{1}
and
X_{\cdot}=X_{\cdot}^{2}
, respectively. To simplify
notation, we denote by
u^{1},X^{1},Z^{1},r^{j,1},\rho^{j,1}
and

u^{2},X^{2},Z^{2},r^{j,2},\rho^{j,2}
the related processes
u,X,Z,r^{j},\rho^{j}.

According to Remark
4.4
, we can use a common space of controls
for the two problems with initial conditions
X_{\cdot}^{1}
or
X_{\cdot}^{2},

namely
L_{\mathcal{F}_{X^{1}X^{2}t}}^{2}(t,T;\mathcal{H}_{m}).

Therefore, we may consider using the control
u^{2}(s)
with the initial condition
X^{1}.

This is suboptimal, and the corresponding trajectory is
X^{2}(s)+X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}
. The suboptimality allows to write the inequality:

\displaystyle\quad V(X_{\cdot}^{1}\otimes m,t)-V(X_{\cdot}^{2}\otimes m,t)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(l(X^{2}(s)+X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2},u^{2}(s))-l(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))\right)dm(x)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left(F\left(((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(s)+X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2})\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)\Bigg]ds

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(h(X^{2}(T)+X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2})-h(X^{2}(T))\right)dm(x)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left(((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(T)+X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2})\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\left\langle l_{x}(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})\right),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{t}^{T}\lambda\Bigg[\left\langle l_{xx}(X^{2}(s)+\lambda\mu(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}),u^{2}(s))(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left\langle D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left(((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(s)+\lambda\mu(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}))\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle\Bigg]dsd\lambda d\mu

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle h_{x}(X^{2}(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\lambda\Bigg[\left\langle h_{xx}(X^{2}(T)+\lambda\mu(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}))(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle

(B.10)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\,+\left\langle D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left(((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(T)+\lambda\mu(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}))\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right)(X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle\Bigg]d\lambda d\mu,

and from the assumptions (
4.5
), (
4.6
), (
4.13
), (
4.14
) we have

(B.11)

\displaystyle V(X_{\cdot}^{1}\otimes m,t)-V(X_{\cdot}^{2}\otimes m,t)\leq\left\langle Z^{2}(t),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle+\dfrac{1}{2}(c_{h}+c_{T}+(c_{l}+c)T)\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rVert^{2}.

By changing the role of
X_{\cdot}^{1}
and
X_{\cdot}^{2}
, we get

(B.12)

V(X^{1}\otimes m,t)-V(X^{2}\otimes m,t)\geq\left\langle Z^{2}(t),X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rangle-\dfrac{1}{2}(c_{h}+c_{T}+(c_{l}+c)T)\left\lVert X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\langle Z^{1}(t)-Z^{2}(t),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle.

Next,

\displaystyle\dfrac{d}{ds}\left\langle X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s),Z^{1}(s)-Z^{2}(s)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s),l_{v}(X^{1}(s),u^{1}(s))-l_{v}(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\bigg\langle X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s),l_{x}(X^{1}(s),u^{1}(s))-l_{x}(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle+\bigg\langle D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{1}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)-D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right),X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\bigg\rangle.

Integrating between
t
and
T
and using (
4.8
), (
4.9
), (
4.15
), (
4.16
)
it follows that

(B.13)

\displaystyle\left\langle Z^{1}(t)-Z^{2}(t),X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\lambda\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds-(c^{\prime}_{l}+c^{\prime})\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds

\displaystyle-(c^{\prime}_{h}+c^{\prime}_{T})\left\lVert X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)\right\rVert^{2}.

But

X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)=X^{1}-X^{2}+\int_{t}^{s}(u^{1}(\tau)-u^{2}(\tau))d\tau,

from which we get

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)\dfrac{T^{2}}{2}\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds+T\left(1+\dfrac{1}{\epsilon}\right)\left\lVert X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rVert^{2},

\displaystyle\left\lVert X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)T\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds+\left(1+\dfrac{1}{\epsilon}\right)\left\lVert X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rVert^{2},

so we obtain the estimate:

(B.14)

\displaystyle\left\langle Z^{1}(t)-Z^{2}(t),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\lambda-T(1+\epsilon)\left((c^{\prime}_{l}+c^{\prime})\dfrac{T}{2}+(c^{\prime}_{h}+c^{\prime}_{T})\right)\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds

\displaystyle-\left(1+\dfrac{1}{\epsilon}\right)((c^{\prime}_{l}+c^{\prime})T+c^{\prime}_{h}+c^{\prime}_{T})\left\lVert X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

From the assumption (
4.19
) and choosing
\epsilon
sufficiently
small, the first term in the right-hand side is positive. Combined
with (
B.12
) and taking account of (
B.11
) it
follows that

(B.15)

\left|V(X^{1}\otimes m,t)-V(X^{2}\otimes m,t)-\left\langle Z^{2}(t),X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rangle\right|\leq C_{T}\left\lVert X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rVert^{2}.

This proves immediately the result (
5.4
). We have also proved
the following estimate:

(B.16)

\alpha\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds\leq\left\langle Z^{1}(t)-Z^{2}(t),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle+\beta\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rVert^{2},

for convenient constants
\alpha,\beta.
Next, we also have

\displaystyle\quad\,\left\langle Z^{1}(t)-Z^{2}(t),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\Bigg\langle\int_{t}^{T}\left[l_{x}(X^{1}(s),u^{1}(s))-l_{x}(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{1}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)-D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)\right]ds

\displaystyle\quad+h_{x}(X^{1}(T))-h_{x}(X^{2}(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{1}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right)-D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left((X_{X_{\cdot}^{2}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right),X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\Bigg\rangle

(B.17)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rVert\Bigg[(c_{l}+c)\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\right\rVert ds+(c_{h}+c_{T})\left\lVert X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)\right\rVert\Bigg],

and

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\right\rVert ds

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rVert+\dfrac{2}{3}\sqrt{T^{3}}\sqrt{\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds},

\displaystyle\left\lVert X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert X^{1}-X^{2}\right\rVert+\sqrt{T}\sqrt{\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds}.

Using these estimates in (
B.17
), combined with (
B.16
),
we obtain

(B.18)

\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds\leq C_{T}\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rVert^{2},

which implies

(B.19)

\displaystyle\left\lVert X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\lVert,

\displaystyle\left\lVert Z^{1}(s)-Z^{2}(s)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\left\lVert u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert r_{X^{1}t}^{j}(s)-r_{X^{2}t}^{j}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{t}^{T}\left\lVert\rho_{X^{1}t}^{j}(s)-\rho_{X^{2}t}^{j}(s)\right\rVert^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\left\lVert X_{\cdot}^{1}-X_{\cdot}^{2}\right\rVert^{2},

and thus (
5.5
) has been proven, which completes the proof.

B.3.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
5.3

We begin with (
5.6
). We have, from the optimality principle
(
4.26
),

(B.20)

\displaystyle V(X\otimes m,t)-V(X\otimes m,t+\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+\epsilon}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}l(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))dm(x)\right)+F\left((Y_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right]ds

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(V\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}},t+\epsilon\right)\right)-V(X\otimes m,t+\epsilon).

Since

(B.21)

\left|\int_{t}^{t+\epsilon}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}l(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))dm(x)\right)+F(Y_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)\right]ds\right|\leq C_{T}\epsilon(1+||X_{\cdot}||^{2}),

it suffices to prove

(B.22)

\left|\mathbb{E}\left(V\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}},t+\epsilon\right)\right)-V(X\otimes m,t+\epsilon)\right|\leq C_{T}\epsilon(1+||X_{\cdot}||^{2}).

But

(B.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(V\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}},t+\epsilon\right)\right)-V(X\otimes m,t+\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigg(l\left(X_{X_{X_{x}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{X_{x}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)\right)-l\left(X_{X_{x},t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{x},t+\epsilon}(s)\right)\bigg)dm(x)

\displaystyle+F\left((X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)-F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\Bigg]ds

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigg(h(X_{X_{X_{x}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(T))-h(X_{X_{x},t+\epsilon}(T))\bigg)dm(x)

\displaystyle+F_{T}\left((X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)-F_{T}\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\Bigg].

Note that we have used the fact
(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}=(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}

and the same with
s=T.

Using Taylor expansion and making use of assumptions (
4.5
), (
4.6
), (
4.13
), (
4.14
),
we can check that

\displaystyle\quad\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\left(V\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}},t+\epsilon\right)\right)-V(X\otimes m,t+\epsilon)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\left\langle l_{v}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)),u_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)-u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\left\langle l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}\right)\right),X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)-X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle h_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{T}}\right)\right),X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(T)-X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T)\right\rangle\Bigg|

(B.24)

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\sup_{t<s<T}\left\lVert X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)-X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\right\rVert^{2}.

Since
X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s),X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)

are the optimal states corresponding to different initial conditions

X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)
and
X_{\cdot}
at initial time
t+\epsilon,

we can use (
B.19
) to see that

(B.25)

\sup_{t<s<T}\left\lVert X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)-X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\right\rVert^{2}\leq C_{T}\left\lVert X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)-X_{\cdot}\right\rVert^{2}\leq C_{T}\epsilon(1+||X_{\cdot}||^{2}).

Next,

(B.26)

X_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(s)-X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)=X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)-X_{\cdot}+\int_{t+\epsilon}^{s}\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}t}^{t+\epsilon}}(\tau)d\tau,

where we have denoted
\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}t}^{t+\epsilon}}(\tau)=u_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(\tau)-u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(\tau).

From the optimality of
u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)
from the problem
with initial condition
X_{\cdot}
at
t+\epsilon
, we can write, by
(
4.21
).

(B.27)

\displaystyle\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\left\langle l_{v}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)),\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}t}^{t+\epsilon}}(s)\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\left\langle l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}\right)\right),\int_{t+\epsilon}^{s}\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}t}^{t+\epsilon}}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle ds

\displaystyle+\left\langle h_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T)+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{T}}\right)\right),\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\widetilde{u}_{\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}t}^{t+\epsilon}}(\tau)d\tau\right\rangle=0.

In addition,

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\left(l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}\right)\right)\right)ds+h_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T))

(B.28)

\displaystyle\quad+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{T}}\right)\right),\sigma(w(t+\epsilon)-w(t))+\beta(b(t+\epsilon)-b(t))\ \Bigg\rangle=0.

Thanks to (
B.27
), (
B.28
) the inequality (
B.24
)
reduces to

(B.29)

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\left(V\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{t+\epsilon}},t+\epsilon\right)\right)-V(X\otimes m,t+\epsilon)

\displaystyle-\Bigg\langle\int_{t+\epsilon}^{T}\left(l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s),u_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{s}}\right)\right)\right)ds

\displaystyle+h_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t+\epsilon}^{T}}\right)\right),\int_{t}^{t+\epsilon}u_{X_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau\ \Bigg\rangle\Bigg|\leq C_{T}\epsilon(1+||X_{\cdot}||^{2}),

from which (
B.22
) follows immediately. We have thus proved
(
5.6
). We turn to (
5.7
), which is equivalent to

(B.30)

||Z_{X,t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)-Z_{Xt}(t)||\leq C_{T}\left(\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+\epsilon||X||\right).

From the BSDE (
4.20
) we can write

Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t)=\mathbb{E}[Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)|\sigma(X)]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\epsilon}\left(l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)\right)ds\bigg|\sigma(X)\right],

and

\displaystyle Z_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)-Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t)

\displaystyle=Z_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)|\sigma(X)]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\epsilon}\left(l_{x}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))+D_{X}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)\right)ds\bigg|\sigma(X)\right].

Therefore,

||Z_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)-Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t)||\leq C_{T}\epsilon(1+||X_{\cdot}||)+||Z_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)-Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)||.

Noting that
Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)=Z_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)

we can write from Proposition
5.2
:

||Z_{X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon),t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)-Z_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)||\leq C_{T}||X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)-X_{\cdot}||,

from which it follows that

|Z_{X_{\cdot},t+\epsilon}(t+\epsilon)-Z_{X_{\cdot}t}(t)||\leq C_{T}\epsilon(1+||X_{\cdot}||)+C_{T}||X_{X_{\cdot}t}(t+\epsilon)-X_{\cdot}||,

and the result (
5.7
) is obtained immediately.

B.4.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
6.1

Proof.

Since

(B.31)

Z_{xmt}(t)=D_{X}V(m,t),

we first need to show that the right hand side is a gradient. For
that purpose, we introduce an ordinary stochastic control parametrized
by the trajectory
X_{xmt}(\cdot)
through the conditional probabilities

(X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}.
We take controls in

L_{\mathcal{F}_{t}}^{2}(t,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the state is defined by

(B.32)

x_{xt}(s)=x+\int_{t}^{s}v(\tau)d\tau+\sigma(w(s)-w(t))+\beta(b(s)-b(t)).

The payoff to minimize is given by

(B.33)

\displaystyle K_{xmt}(v(\cdot))=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}l(x_{xt}(s),v(s))ds\right)+\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)(x_{xt}(s))\right)ds

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}(h(x_{xt}(T)))+\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(x_{xt}(T))\right).

If we write the necessary conditions of optimality, it is easy to
check that the optimal control coincides with
u_{xmt}(s)
and the
optimal state is
X_{xmt}(s).
So

(B.34)

\inf_{v(\cdot)}K_{xmt}(v(\cdot))=K_{xmt}(u_{xmt}(\cdot)),

which is the right-hand side of (
6.15
). We set
\Psi(x,m,t)=K_{xmt}(u_{xmt}(\cdot)).

So we need to prove that

(B.35)

Z_{xmt}(t)=D\Psi(x,m,t).

This will be a consequence of the estimate

(B.36)

|\Psi(x_{1},m,t)-\Psi(x_{2},m,t)-Z_{x_{2}mt}(t)\cdot(x_{1}-x_{2})|\leq C_{T}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}.

The proof has similarities with that of Proposition
5.2
,
although it is simpler. To simplify notation, we set

u^{1}(s)=u_{x_{1}mt}(s),\ u^{2}(s)=u_{x_{2}mt}(s),\ X^{1}(s)=X_{x_{1}mt}(s),\ X^{2}(s)=X_{x_{2}mt}(s).

If we use the control
u^{2}(s)
with initial condition
x_{1},

to get the state
X^{2}(s)+x_{1}-x_{2,}
it is suboptimal, so we
can write:

(B.37)

\displaystyle\quad\,\Psi(x_{1},m,t)-\Psi(x_{2},m,t)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\left(l(X^{2}(s)+x_{1}-x_{2},u^{2}(s))-l(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\,\,+\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)(X^{2}(s)+x_{1}-x_{2})-\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)(X^{2}(s))\right)\bigg]ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{E}\left(h(X^{2}(T)+x_{1}-x_{2})-h(X^{2}(T))\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{E}\left(\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X^{2}(T)+x_{1}-x_{2})-\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\left((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X^{2}(T))\right).

Using Taylor expansion and making use of the assumptions, we obtain
the estimate:

(B.38)

\Psi(x_{1},m,t)-\Psi(x_{2},m,t)\leq Z_{x_{2}mt}(t)\cdot(x_{1}-x_{2})+C|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}.

By interchanging
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
and rearranging we have also

(B.39)

\Psi(x_{1},m,t)-\Psi(x_{2},m,t)\geq Z_{x_{2}mt}(t)\cdot(x_{1}-x_{2})+(Z_{x_{1}mt}(t)-Z_{x_{2}mt}(t))(x_{1}-x_{2})-C|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}.

The result will follow from the estimate:

(B.40)

(Z_{x_{1}mt}(t)-Z_{x_{2}mt}(t))(x_{1}-x_{2})\geq-C_{T}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}.

To obtain (
B.40
), observe that

\displaystyle\quad(Z^{1}(t)-Z^{2}(t))\cdot(x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(l_{v}(X^{1}(s),u^{1}(s))-l_{v}(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))\Big)\cdot(u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\bigg(l_{x}(X^{1}(s),u^{1}(s))-l_{x}(X^{2}(s),u^{2}(s))+\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X^{1}(s))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}})(X^{2}(s))\bigg)\cdot(X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s))\bigg]\Bigg\}ds

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{E}\bigg(h_{x}(X^{1}(T))-h_{x}(X^{2}(T))+\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X^{1}(T))

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\,\,\,-\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}})(X^{2}(T))\cdot(X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T))\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\lambda\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}|u^{1}(s)-u^{2}(s)|^{2}ds\right)-(c^{\prime}_{l}+c^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}|X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)|^{2}ds\right)-(c^{\prime}_{h}+c^{\prime}_{T})\mathbb{E}\left(|X^{1}(T)-X^{2}(T)|^{2}\right).

Since

X^{1}(s)-X^{2}(s)=x_{1}-x_{2}+\int_{t}^{s}(u^{1}(\tau)-u^{2}(\tau))d\tau,

it is straightforward to complete the calculations, and thanks to (
4.19
)
we obtain (
B.40
) and then (
B.36
). So
\Psi(x,m,t)

is Lipschitz in
x.
Let us check that it is Lipschitz in
m.
We
give the main results but skip the details. The key point is to establish
the following formula:

\displaystyle\quad\Psi(x,m^{\prime},t)-\Psi(x,m,t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{t}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\Bigg[\theta l_{xx}\Big(\delta_{(\theta\mu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s),\delta_{(\theta\mu)}u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\theta l_{xv}\Big(\delta_{(\theta\mu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s),\delta_{(\theta\mu)}u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\Big(u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\theta l_{vv}\Big(\delta_{(\theta\mu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s),\delta_{(\theta\mu)}u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\Big(u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\cdot\Big(u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\theta D^{2}\dfrac{dF}{d\nu}\Big((\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\Big)\Big(\delta_{(\theta\mu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\Bigg]d\theta d\mu ds\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\Bigg[\theta h_{xx}\Big(\delta_{(\theta\mu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(T)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(T)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\theta D^{2}\dfrac{dF_{T}}{d\nu}\Big((\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\Big)\Big(\delta_{(\theta\mu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(T)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(T)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\Bigg]d\theta d\mu\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\left[\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\left(D_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}\Big(\delta_{(\theta)}\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s),\delta_{(\mu)}X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(s)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}^{1}t}^{1}(s)-X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(s)\Big)\right)\right]d\theta d\mu ds\right)

(B.41)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\left[\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\left(D_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F_{T}}{d\nu^{2}}\Big(\delta_{(\theta)}\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(T),\delta_{(\mu)}X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(T)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}^{1}t}^{1}(T)-X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(T)\Big)\right)\right]d\theta d\mu\right),

where

(B.42)

\begin{split}\delta_{(\theta\mu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)&:=X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)+\theta\mu\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big),\\
\delta_{(\theta\mu)}u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)&:=u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)+\theta\mu\Big(u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big),\\
\delta_{(\theta)}\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)&:=(\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}+\theta\Big((\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}-(\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s))^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\Big),\\
\delta_{(\mu)}X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(s)&:=X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(s)+\mu\Big(X_{\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}^{1}t}^{1}(s)-X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(s)\Big).\end{split}

Interchanging
m
with
m^{\prime}
and adding up, we obtain, using the assumptions
of Section
4.2
,

(B.43)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left(\lambda\left|u_{x\,\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}-(c^{\prime}_{l}+c^{\prime})\left|X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle-(c^{\prime}_{h}+c^{\prime}_{T})\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(T)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{t}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\bigg(DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}\Big(\delta_{(\theta)}\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\Big(\delta_{(\nu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s),\delta_{(\mu)}X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}\,t}}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}^{1}t}^{1}(s)-X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(s)\Big)\bigg)d\theta d\mu d\nu ds\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}^{1\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\bigg(DD_{1}\dfrac{d^{2}F}{d\nu^{2}}\Big(\delta_{(\theta)}\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\Big(\delta_{(\nu)}X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T),\delta_{(\mu)}X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(T)\Big)

\displaystyle\Big(X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(T)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\Big)\cdot\Big(X_{\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}^{1}t}^{1}(T)-X_{\widehat{X}_{m}^{1}t}^{1}(T)\Big)\bigg)d\theta d\mu d\nu\Bigg).

Using

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left|X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{(T-t)^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left|u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}ds\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(T)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(T)\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(T-t)\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left|u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}ds\right),

we deduce after simple estimates

(B.44)

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left|u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}ds\right)\leq C_{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}-\widehat{X}_{m}\right|^{2}\right)=C_{T}W_{2}^{2}(m,m^{\prime}),

(B.45)

\sup_{s\in(t,T)}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)-X_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}\right),\sup_{s\in(t,T)}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|u_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)-u_{x\widehat{X}_{m}t}(s)\right|^{2}\right)\leq C_{T}W_{2}^{2}(m,m^{\prime}),\text{ and}

(B.46)

\sup_{s\in(t,T)}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X_{x\widehat{X}_{m^{\prime}}t}(s)\right|^{2}\right)\leq C_{T}(1+W_{2}^{2}(m,\delta)).

Collecting estimates, we can assert that

(B.47)

|\Psi(x,m^{\prime},t)-\Psi(x,m,t)|\leq C_{T}W_{2}^{2}(m,m^{\prime})+K_{T}(m)W_{2}(m,m^{\prime}),

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
PROOFS FROM SECTION
7

C.1.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
7.1

The control problem (
7.5
), (
7.6
) is
linear quadratic. One can check that it is strictly convex and has
a unique minimum. The optimal control is linear in
y
and can be
written
\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)y
, where
\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)
is
defined by (
7.1
), (
7.2
), (
7.3
).
Define

u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)=\dfrac{u_{x+\epsilon y,mt}(s)-u_{xmt}(s)}{\varepsilon},\,X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)=\dfrac{X_{x+\epsilon y,mt}(s)-X_{xmt}(s)}{\varepsilon},

(C.1)

Z_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)=\dfrac{Z_{x+\epsilon y,mt,t}(s)-Z_{xmt}(s)}{\epsilon}\ ,r_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s)=\dfrac{r_{x+\epsilon y,mt}^{j}(s)-r_{xmt}^{j}(s)}{\epsilon},

\rho_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s)=\dfrac{\rho_{x+\epsilon y,mt}^{j}(s)-\rho_{xmt}^{j}(s)}{\epsilon},

then we have the relations:

(C.2)

X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)=y+\int_{t}^{s}u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(\tau)d\tau,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\bigg[l_{vx}\Big(X_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),u_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)\Big)X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)

(C.3)

\displaystyle\qquad+l_{vv}\Big(X_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),u_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)\Big)u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)\bigg]d\theta+Z_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)=0,

\displaystyle-dZ_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s))=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\bigg[l_{xx}\Big(X_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),u_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)\Big)X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)

(C.4)

\displaystyle\qquad+l_{xv}\Big(X_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),u_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)\Big)u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)\bigg]d\theta

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}F\left((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\Big(X_{xmt}(s)+\theta\epsilon X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)\Big)X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)d\theta

\displaystyle-\sum_{j}r_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s)dw_{j}(s)-\sum_{j}\rho_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s)db_{j}(s),

(C.5)

\displaystyle Z_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(T)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}h_{xx}\Big(X_{xmt}(T)+\theta\epsilon X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(T)\Big)X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(T)d\theta

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}F\left((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\Big(X_{xmt}(T)+\theta\epsilon X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(T)\Big)X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(T)d\theta.

By techniques already used, we can check that
u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),

X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),Z_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)
are bounded in
L_{\mathcal{F}_{t}}^{\infty}(t,T;\mathcal{H}_{m})

and
r_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s),\rho_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s)
are bounded
in
L_{\mathcal{F}_{t}}^{2}(t,T;\mathcal{H}_{m}).

We consider then subsequences of
u_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),

X_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s),Z_{xymt}^{\epsilon}(s)
,r_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s),\rho_{xymt}^{j,\epsilon}(s)

which converge weakly in
L_{\mathcal{F}_{t}}^{2}(t,T;\mathcal{H}_{m})

to
\mathcal{U}_{xymt}(s),\mathcal{X}_{xymt}(s),\mathcal{Z}_{xymt}(s),\mathcal{R}_{xymt}^{j}(s),\Theta_{xymt}^{j}(s).

It follows easily that the limit is
\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)y
,
\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(s)y
,
\mathcal{Z}_{xmt}(s)y
,
\mathcal{R}_{xmt}^{j}(s)y
,
\Theta_{xmt}^{j}(s)y
.
It is possible to show that the convergence is strong using standard techniques. This completes
the proof.

C.2.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
7.2

By calculations already done, we can check the relation

(C.6)

\displaystyle y\cdot\mathcal{Z}_{xmt}(t)y

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\int_{t}^{T}\Bigg\{\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)y\cdot l_{vv}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)y

\displaystyle+2\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)y\cdot l_{vx}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(s)y

\displaystyle+\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(s)y\cdot\bigg(l_{xx}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))

\displaystyle+D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}F\left((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)(X_{xmt}(s))\bigg)\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(s)y\Bigg\}ds\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(T)y\cdot\left(h_{xx}(X_{xmt}(T))+D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}F\left((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X_{xmt}(T))\right)\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(T)y\right).

Also,

(C.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{xmt}(t)y

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t}^{T}\bigg(\left(l_{xx}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))+D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}F\left((X_{\cdot mt}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)(X_{xmt}(s))\right)\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(s)y

\displaystyle+l_{xv}(X_{xmt}(s),u_{xmt}(s))\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)y)\bigg)ds

\displaystyle+\left(h_{xx}(X_{xmt}(T))+D^{2}\dfrac{d}{d\nu}F\left((X_{\cdot mt}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)(X_{xmt}(T))\right)\mathcal{X}_{xmt}(T)y\bigg].

From the assumptions and estimates already done, we deduce
\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{T}|\mathcal{U}_{xmt}(s)y|^{2}ds\right)\leq C_{T}|y|^{2}.

It easily follows
|\mathcal{Z}_{xmt}(t)y|\leq C_{T}
. This concludes
the proof.

C.3.
PROOF OF PROPOSITION
7.3

We connect the system (
7.10
)
to a control problem. The space of controls is
L_{\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}}^{2}(t,T;\mathcal{H}_{m})

where
\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}
is the filtration generated
by the
\sigma
-algebras
\mathcal{F}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}^{s}
.
If
\mathcal{V}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
is a control, the state
is defined by

(C.8)

\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)=\mathcal{X}+\int_{t}^{s}\mathcal{V}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\tau)d\tau,

and the payoff is

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\mathcal{V}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{T}\Bigg[\left\langle l_{xx}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)+D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)(\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)),\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\,+2\left\langle l_{xv}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{V}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s),\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle+\left\langle l_{vv}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{V}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s),\mathcal{\mathcal{V}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle\Bigg]ds

(C.9)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\dfrac{1}{2}\left\langle h_{xx}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T)+D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F_{T}\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(T)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{T}}\right)\right)(\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T)),\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(T)\right\rangle.

Thanks to the assumption (
4.19
) this is a linear quadratic
convex problem, which has a unique optimal control. The system
(
7.10
) has a unique
solution and the optimal control is
\mathcal{U}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
. The
optimal state is
\mathcal{\mathcal{X}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)
.
Moreover, one has

(C.10)

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathcal{V}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\cdot)}\mathcal{J}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\mathcal{V}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\cdot))=\dfrac{1}{2}\langle\mathcal{Z}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(t),\mathcal{X}\rangle

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{T}\Bigg[\left\langle l_{xx}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)+D_{X}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(F\left((X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s)\otimes m)^{\mathcal{B}_{t}^{s}}\right)\right)(\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)),\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle

\displaystyle+2\left\langle l_{xv}(X_{X_{\cdot}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{U}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(\tau),\mathcal{X}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle+\left\langle l_{vv}(X_{X_{{}_{\cdot}}t}(s),u_{X_{\cdot}t}(s))\mathcal{U}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s),\mathcal{\mathcal{U}}_{X_{\cdot}\mathcal{X}_{\cdot}t}(s)\right\rangle\Bigg]ds
